# Wardogs: Weapon Attachments and Platforms

> **Project:** [`Kiyogitpy/wardogs-data-docs`](https://github.com/Kiyogitpy/wardogs-data-docs) · [Live site](https://kiyogitpy.github.io/wardogs-data-docs/)

*Sources: every `ID_*` item definition under [`data/`](https://github.com/Kiyogitpy/wardogs-data-docs/tree/main/data) that carries a `WDInventoryFragment_Attachment`, the weapon items of [`Weapons/Platforms`](https://github.com/Kiyogitpy/wardogs-data-docs/tree/main/data/Wardogs/Plugins/GameFeatures/Weapons/Platforms) and the legacy weapon folders, the slot archetype assets under [`Content/Items/Attachments/Archetypes`](https://github.com/Kiyogitpy/wardogs-data-docs/tree/main/data/Wardogs/Content/Items/Attachments/Archetypes), and the effect modifiers in [`assets/data/ge-values.json`](https://github.com/Kiyogitpy/wardogs-data-docs/blob/main/assets/data/ge-values.json). Every table is generated by [`_tools/attachment_tables.py`](https://github.com/Kiyogitpy/wardogs-data-docs/blob/main/_tools/attachment_tables.py); do not edit one by hand.*

This page is the inventory: what attachment items exist, what each one changes, which weapon slots take it, and what slots each weapon has. Item loading, sub-slot filters and default loadouts come from the same code as [Weapon Attributes](../content/Wardogs_WeaponAttributes_v1.md), so the two agree. That page adds the rules for combining modifiers and the final value of every attribute per weapon; this page does not repeat them. Prices, unlock levels and shop text are in [Weapon Shop and Parts](Wardogs_WeaponShop_v1.md). The Gunsmith screens are in [Gunsmith](Wardogs_GameFeatures_Gunsmith_v2.md). To try a loadout, use the [Weapon Loadout Calculator](../content/loadout-calculator.md).

Labels: **Data** is read straight from the export. **Derived** is worked out from Data by the generator (fit lists, counts, ranges). **Inferred** is a reading the data does not state. Audio is out of scope; a tag that only names an audio cue is shown as a tag and not explained.

<!-- attachment_tables counts -->
- Attachment items (`WDInventoryFragment_Attachment` with attachment data): **298**, in 8 attachment types.
- Items with an attachment fragment whose `AttachmentData` is null: **6** (listed under section 7).
- Slot archetype assets: **12**.
- Weapon platforms (ranged weapons with base attributes): **38**.
- Attachments that change nothing in data (no weapon or character effect): **148**.
- Attachments no weapon sub-slot accepts, to three nesting levels: **52**.
- Gameplay effects in `ge-values.json` reached by an attachment: **254**.
<!-- /attachment_tables -->

## 1. How an attachment is built

**Data.** An attachment is an item definition (`BHInventoryItemDefinition`) with a `WDInventoryFragment_Attachment`. Its `AttachmentData` holds:

| Field | What it does | Where it appears below |
|---|---|---|
| `AttachmentType` | the slot tag the item belongs to (`Item.Attachment.Barrel`, ...) | the type groups of sections 5 and 6 |
| `AppliedEffects` | gameplay effects applied to the weapon while the item is fitted | "Weapon-side modifiers" |
| `AppliedCharacterEffects` | gameplay effects applied to the character holding the weapon | "Character-side modifiers" |
| `SightsData` (sights) | one effect list per sight name | `Sight` lines |
| bipod lists (bipods) | unfolded and mounted lists for the weapon and the character | `Bipod` lines |
| `bCanBeRemovedInGame`, `bDiscardWhenEmptyOnReload` | removal and reload flags | section 5 |
| type-specific fields | reload animation rate (magazines), muzzle flash scale and cue tag (muzzles), stance limits (bipods) | "Other data", section 6.2 |

The item also carries a weight (`BHInventoryFragment_Weight`), a grid shape (`BHInventoryFragment_CustomShape`), context tags (`ItemContext`) and, for barrels and handguards, its own sub-slots (`BHInventoryFragment_SubSlotSupport`), for example a barrel's `Muzzle` slot. A weapon has the same sub-slot list (section 4). Whether an item fits a slot is decided by the slot's filter: an allow list, a block list, or both, over item ids and context tags ([Weapon Attributes, Choices](../content/Wardogs_WeaponAttributes_v1.md#choices-this-page-makes), native `0x67A2B40`). Context tags match the tag itself or a child tag.

## 2. Slot archetypes

**Data.** One archetype asset per slot tag: the bone the part attaches to and a category. The category is not serialized on the assets that show "(not serialized)" (it is the native default, not in the export). The Mount* and Underbarrel slots have no attachment item of their own type in this export: the underbarrel slot is filled with `Grip` and `Bipod` items (section 4). Bipods have no archetype asset.

## 3. Weapon platforms

**Data.** One row per ranged weapon with base attributes. Grid is the inventory shape (`X x Y`). Caliber is the chamber slot's allowed `Item.Caliber.*` context. "Clearable in Gunsmith" is `ClearableAttachmentSlots` of the weapon's Gunsmith fragment: the slots the player may empty on the Gunsmith screen (**Inferred** from the name).

## 4. Attachment slots per platform

**Data and Derived.** One table per weapon, in the order the weapon lists its sub-slots. A row `↳` is a sub-slot that the default item in the slot above brings (the default barrel's muzzle slot, the default magazine's ammo slot). Columns:

- **Hidden**: `bIsHiddenContainer` (**Inferred**: not shown as a pick list; the chamber, ammo and most fixed parts are hidden).
- **Filter / Rule**: the filter method and its lists. `AllowList` admits only listed items and tags; `BlockPriority` admits what is allowed and not blocked (an empty allow list admits nothing); `BlockList` admits all but the blocked.
- **Default item**: the item the slot starts with (`DefaultContainerItems`, else `DefaultItems`, first real item).
- **Accepted items**: every attachment item that passes this slot's filter, by asset name without `ID_`. Nested rows show only the sub-slot of the default parent item; other barrels and handguards bring their own muzzle and underbarrel slots, and the Fits column of section 5 lists those.

<!-- /attachment_tables -->

## 5. Attachment items

**Data and Derived.** All attachment items grouped by `AttachmentType`. "Context tags" omits `Item.Context.Equippable` (on every item) and the type tag itself. "Brings sub-slots" lists the item's own sub-slots and their filter method. **Fits** is Derived: the slot and the weapons that accept the item, on the weapon itself or on a sub-slot of an attachment it accepts (`Barrel>Muzzle`), to three nesting levels, with the filter rules above. Weight units are not stated in the data; an empty cell means the item has no weight or grid fragment.

## 6. What each attachment changes

### 6.1 Effects

**Data.** Modifiers are resolved from `ge-values.json`. Each cell lists the gameplay effect and its modifiers as `Attribute op value`; attribute names drop `Attribute.Gameplay.`. Operations: `+` Add, `×` Multiply, `÷` Divide, `×c` MultiplyCompound, `+f` AddFinal, `=` Override. "current X" is a modifier backed by another attribute's current value (the seven sight effects that override `MaxZeroingRange` with `MinZeroingRange`). Weapon-side modifiers change the weapon's attributes. Character-side modifiers go to the character instead (aim sway, weapon sway stabilization). How the modifiers combine into a final value, including stacking and the sight and bipod rules, is in [Weapon Attributes, Rules](../content/Wardogs_WeaponAttributes_v1.md#rules) and the [Gameplay Effects Pipeline](../pipelines/Wardogs_GameplayEffectsPipeline_v1.md). A type group lists only the items with an effect or type-specific data; the rest are in section 7.

The "Other data" column carries the type-specific fields. A shotgun choke muzzle that sets `bOverrideShotgunData` replaces the weapon's shotgun pellet-cone data; the replacement values are not tabulated here.

### 6.2 Bipod stance limits

**Data.** Each bipod's `StanceData`: how far the weapon may turn while mounted and where the bipod looks for a surface, per stance. Lock angles are in degrees (**Inferred** from the field names); "off" means the lock flag is false. `bTryMountOnUnfold` is true only for Prone on every bipod. Every bipod also has `BipodMountedSurfaceTraceRange` 8 and `BipodMountLocationSocketName` `S_BipodMountLocation`; the further per-stance trace distances are in each item's export.

## 7. Attachments that change nothing, and attachments nobody accepts

**Derived.** An attachment "changes nothing in data" when it has no weapon or character effect in any list. This holds for all barrels, stocks and handguards and most grips and magazines. **Inferred:** these parts are structural or cosmetic (the part the weapon is built from, the model on screen); the data states no effect and none was traced. It does not exclude an effect in native code that reads the item's tags or weight rather than an effect list.

<!-- /attachment_tables -->

### 7.1 Why 52 attachments fit no weapon

**Derived.** *Evidence: `data/` filters evaluated with the native allow/block rule of `0x67A2B40`; nothing was run.* The 52 are not a nesting-depth effect. Each was also tested against every sub-slot on every weapon and every attachment at any depth, and against the slots of every non-weapon item (tools, deployable triggers, vehicle storage and backpacks excluded as they accept anything), and the result is the same: only four fit any slot at all, and those four fit tool slots, not weapons. So the three-level limit of the fit column cannot be the cause. The rest, by cause (counts from the table below, recomputed by the generator):

- **Tool and deployable ammo (4).** `ID_Battery` and `ID_LrgBattery` fit the `Magazine` slot of the drills, the defibrillator and the range finder; `ID_C4_Explosive` and `ID_IED_Explosive` fit the `Chamber` slot of their triggers. They are attachment-typed magazines of non-weapon items, not unused content.
- **The owner weapon has no slot of that type (9).** The part belongs to a weapon whose model carries it fixed or internal: handguards on the M4, CGM4, AK74M, WEPN_028 and Glock 17, the internal magazines of the combat bow and MP43, the Launcher_04 magazine, and the grip of the Mosin. These items still exist as a model or a cosmetic option: `ID_HNDG_003` (WEPN_028), `ID_M4Handguard` (M4) and `ID_ZenitHandguard` (AK74M, WEPN_031, SMG_03) are also named by the weapon files as handguard mesh options (a `MutableParameterToUse` rule keyed on `Item.Attachment.Handguard`), which is a visual choice, not an attachment slot.
- **Slot filter names a replacement (9).** The weapon has the slot but its allow list names a different item: the 5.56 weapons (M4, A91, TAR21, KH2002) take only the three STANAG magazines, so the per-weapon `ID_A91_Mag_Standard`, `ID_TAR21_Mag_Standard`, `ID_KH2002_Mag_Standard`/`_Extended` and `ID_M4_Mag_Extended` are superseded; M249 takes `M249Barrel` (not `ID_LMG_01_BRL_06`), Vector takes `VectorBarrel` and `VectorStock` (not `ID_SMG_01_BRL_03`, `ID_RS2Stock`) and the Mosin takes `MosinStock` (not `ID_SR_02_STK_01`). The vendor table `DT_WeaponMarket` still lists `ID.Item.A91Mag` and `ID.Item.TAR21Mag` as faction-specific magazine rows, so those two can be sold but cannot be fitted by the filters as exported (**unconfirmed** whether the shop or another path adds them to a weapon at run time).
- **No weapon is named for it (30).** The numbered parts `ID_HNDG_005` to `ID_HNDG_016`, `ID_STOK_006` to `ID_STOK_012`, `ID_PGRP_006` to `ID_PGRP_009`, `ID_TRGR_002`/`003`, `ID_BARL_011`, `ID_SR_02_BRL_04`, `ID_SR_04_BRL_05`, `ID_SR_04_GRP_03` and `ID_CantedIrons`. Across all 52, 30 appear in `data/` only in the UI item catalog `DT_EntityData_UI_Items` and 12 appear nowhere else at all (the generated table shows each item's references). **Inferred:** parts that were cut or never wired into a weapon. The data carries no season, deprecation or disabled marker on any of the 52 (checked for those words in each item file), so season gating is **not supported**; neither is it excluded for rules held in native code.

**Not found:** a runtime path that places one of the 47 non-tool items on a weapon; none is visible from data. `ID_CantedIrons` is the only one of the 52 with an effect (sight entry `S_AimCanted1`); it never applies on any weapon in the data.

They are not attachment items in the sense of sections 5 and 6: no type, no effects, no slot of their own. They are listed in [Weapon Shop and Parts](Wardogs_WeaponShop_v1.md) with their shop rows.

## Unexplained and not traced

- **Attachments with no effect.** Section 7 lists every item whose data names no effect. Whether any of them acts through code (for example the magazine capacity or the part model) is not traced. Magazine capacity itself is not stored on the magazine item in this export.
- **`ID_MUZL_064`** is a suppressor-tagged muzzle (cue tag, flash scale 0.2) with no effect list at all; the other suppressors name effects. Nothing in the export says what it changes.
- **Suppressor muzzles** (21 items) carry the cue tag `Attachment.Cue.Suppressor` and attach and detach UI sound tags. What the tag changes beyond sound is not stated in the data and not traced here (audio is out of scope).
- **Obfuscated magazine fields (consumer found).** Two obfuscated float fields next to `ReloadAnimationPlayRate` on magazines (`FWDMagazineAttachmentData` `+0x68` `_aaabcedhgdocfgafjkign` and `+0x6C` `_aaabfbhbgfmafmfmaajnd`) hold the same value on every item: 1.0 on 66 magazines, 0.7 on `ID_LMG_02_Magazine_Standard`, 0.6 on `ID_M249_Mag_Standard`, 0.8 on `ID_MAGZ_062`. **Native, derived (confidence high):** they are time multipliers for the inventory ammo-packing operations, not for the combat reload. The struct's typed accessor is `0x6C3AB00` (it checks the item's attachment data against the `WDMagazineAttachmentData` struct, whose static-struct getter is `0x6C43560`, cached at RVA `0xD36D628`; that getter has no other caller) and two thin getters return the fields, 1.0 when the item has no magazine data: `0x6C3B070` returns `+0x68`, `0x6C3B040` returns `+0x6C`. A byte scan of the whole `.text` for `call`/`jmp rel32` finds no other callers of the four functions (getter `0x6C3B040` called at `0x6B5E586`, `0x6B5E7FA`; getter `0x6C3B070` at `0x6B5E5F0`, `0x6C47797`). The consumers: (1) `0x6B5E500`, called twice by the native body of `UWDPlayerInventoryComponent::CombineAmmo` (`0x6B554B0`, calls at `0x6B55E4D` and `0x6B56651`; the exec thunk `0x6B67D00` jumps to it), returns **`curveA(rounds) * field(+0x6C) of one magazine + curveB(rounds) * field(+0x68) of the other`**; the first term only when its flag argument is set (the first call at `0x6B55E4D` passes flag 0 and no first magazine). `curveA` and `curveB` come from the inventory component's `UWDInventoryMagPackingData` (component `+0xB20`; the asset is [`DA_InventoryMagPackingData`](https://github.com/Kiyogitpy/wardogs-data-docs/blob/main/data/Wardogs/Content/GameMechanics/Inventory/InventoryMagPacking/DA_InventoryMagPackingData.json)): a per-caliber-tag override map (`+0x1C8` and `+0x218`, searched up the gameplay-tag parents by `0x6C2F100`) or the default curve (`+0x30` and `+0xB8`, both `CR_CombineAmmoCurve`, 1.0 at 0 rounds to 6.0 at 100), evaluated at the round count in `0x6C26830`. (2) `0x6B5E790` multiplies the first-curve value by `+0x6C` for one magazine; its caller is `0x754B640` (called at `0x754BBCF`), which divides the result by the round count to get a per-round interval (class of that caller not resolved). (3) The native body `0x6C47420` of vtable slot 86 of `UWDCustomInventoryOperationRule_LoadAmmo` multiplies the second-curve value (`+0x218` map / `+0xB8` default) by `+0x68` of the magazine item (call `0x6C47797`). So a value below 1 (0.6 on the M249 magazine, 0.7 on the LMG_02 magazine, 0.8 on `MAGZ_062`) shortens the time to pack rounds into or out of that magazine in the inventory, and 1.0 leaves the curve time unchanged. **Derived:** both fields carry the same value on each item, so the two roles are scaled equally. **Not traced:** which of the two magazines in `CombineAmmo` is the source or the target (unconfirmed), and whether the per-caliber override curves matter (only `Id.Item.545mm` has keys in the asset; the other 11 entries are empty). Search: whole `.text` byte scans for the four call targets and for the static-struct cache global; the struct's reflection registration; `B_Action_Reload` listing (reads only `bDiscardWhenEmptyOnReload` from the base attachment data and takes reload speed from the character attribute `ReloadSpeedMultiplier`, so the combat reload does not read these fields). `ReloadAnimationPlayRate` itself (`+0x50`) has no native consumer found by the same typed accessor route and is not in the dumped reload listing (open).
- **Shotgun choke muzzles** (`bOverrideShotgunData`) change the pellet-cone data of the weapon. Only the flag is shown.
- **Unused slot archetypes.** `MountLeft`, `MountRight`, `MountTop`, `MountUnder` and `Underbarrel` have archetype assets but no attachment item of that type. Whether the game uses them is not traced.
- **Fit is derived from the filters**, not from a game table; the shipped client may add runtime rules not seen in the data. Attachments no weapon accepts are explained in section 7.1 (not a depth effect; tool slots, missing slots, replaced items and unwired parts); a run-time path that fits one is not seen in the data.
- Prices, shop text and unlock levels are not on this page; see [Weapon Shop and Parts](Wardogs_WeaponShop_v1.md).
